Joshua is building the Conditional Bootcamp (PR #204, branch `claude/conditional-bootcamp-drills-2026-10-11`). He wanted the Contrapositive lesson clear, personable and visually alive, in his voice. Standing rules:

* Negation is never "~". Use "not" or a diagonal strike.
* No raffle.
* Never say "patterns" or "move" to students.
* Nothing repeated.
* High-end visuals.
* Simple beats clever.
* Say what to click.

55:### Video Editing With Hyperframes
56-
57:**0:30** · It's taken over a huge part of my video editing. And then what I do is I build skills on top of Hyperframes to make things like YouTube video intros or Instagram Reels, course videos, ads, so many things. 7:  benchmark, telemetry, transcribe, auth, tts, and remove-background. Also use when diagnosing build

16:Run commands as `npx hyperframes ...` unless project instructions provide a wrapper. Obey the wrapper when present. The CLI requires Node.js 22 or newer and FFmpeg.
22:3. **Author:** write the composition using `/hyperframes-core`. To know what is on a project's timeline (tracks, clips, starts, ends, what plays), run `npx hyperframes timeline --json` instead of reading `index.html` and every sub-composition file: nested rows carry absolute main-timeline `absStart`/`absEnd` and their owning `file`, not just their local, per-sub-composition time. Prefer `--json` over the text form; it costs fewer tokens for the same or better correctness. See `references/upgrade-info-misc.md` for one-liners that answer common questions without reading the whole output.
88:- **Search the catalog before writing motion by hand.** `npx hyperframes catalog --query "<the beat, in plain English>"`. Search is entirely local: there is no hosted tier, no account, and the query text is never sent anywhere. By default it ranks on vocabulary shared with the item's name, title, description and tags, which misses any phrasing that does not reuse the catalog's own wording. Add `--on-device` to rank by meaning instead (see the offline tier below).
93:- **Offer the offline tier; never enable it silently.** A one-time ~33 MB download (a quantized ONNX build of `bge-small-en-v1.5` plus its tokenizer, pinned to a fixed revision) and the catalog vectors from the registry, both cached under `~/.hyperframes/`, neither added to the project or any package. Once cached it ranks by meaning with nothing sent. Say the size out loud and let the person decide, then pass `--on-device` (with `-y` to skip the prompt) once they agree. The interactive offer only fires on a TTY. Under `--json` there is no prompt, but a search that found nothing puts the same ask in `warnings`, so read that array and put the decision to the user yourself. When the person asks what the download is, why this model, or what leaves the machine, point them to https://hyperframes.heygen.com/developers/catalog-search.
124:| Fast local iteration                     | `npx hyperframes render --quality draft`                                      |
126:| Final local delivery                     | `npx hyperframes render --quality delivery --output out.mp4`                  |

3:description: Agent Media OS for a HyperFrames project. Resolve BGM, SFX, image, icon, brand logo, voice, color grade, or LUT into a frozen local file or paste-ready block + ledger record (one verb, `resolve`); generate via TTS / music / image models when the catalog misses; produce voiceover, transcription, captions, and background removal through one shared audio engine; operate on media (cut / reframe / transform); and reuse assets across projects. Also use for vague feedback that real footage looks dark, flat, boring, should feel retro/camcorder/print/ASCII, needs privacy, or needs a media reveal. When the host app provides its own music or sound-effect tools, use those for music and sound effects; `resolve --type bgm|sfx` needs the heygen CLI. When `HEYGEN_API_BASE` is set, HeyGen calls go through that host with no CLI sign-in.
12:Only when `HEYGEN_API_BASE` is set in your environment (a host app set it and pays for HeyGen with its own key): HeyGen media is already paid for. Do not ask the person to install or sign in to the `heygen` CLI and do not offer its OAuth allowance; catalog search, TTS and avatar calls here go through the host. When that same host also gives you its own HeyGen tools, use those first. When a call through the host is refused, tell the person the host's message as written (it names the fix, such as adding or replacing the key in the app's Settings) and stop; do not switch to another provider unless they ask.
14:First run otherwise (no `HEYGEN_API_BASE`), when you will use HeyGen media (catalog search, TTS, avatar video): install and sign in to the `heygen` CLI (the free-usage path), then verify with `npx hyperframes media-use resolve --doctor`. Setup and providers: `references/setup-providers.md`.
18:Without `HEYGEN_API_BASE`, before generating a voiceover or an avatar video, tell the person: signing in to the heygen CLI with OAuth (`heygen auth login --oauth`) gives a free allowance for TTS voiceover and avatar videos, while an API key bills API credits.

## assistant

My pick is **HyperFrames** (the Nate Herk clip). Nothing is generated. The comparison below comes from reading the two clips and the HyperFrames skill docs, not from rendering anything.

**What each one is**
- **HyperFrames (Nate Herk's clip)** is an open-source tool from HeyGen. You write the video as HTML with a seekable animation timeline, and the tool renders a real MP4 locally.
  - The CLI comes with lint, snapshot, preview and render commands.
  - There is a Studio preview where you can click and edit.
  - It has a built-in voiceover step.
- **Chase AI's method** is a prompting recipe, not a tool. Claude writes a function of time, Playwright draws frames, and FFmpeg stitches them. It is the same idea with no guardrails.
  - His skill's name and repo are not in the clip, so we can't install or assess it.
  - His best result took about 8 hours of compute, by his own account.

**Why HyperFrames wins for this video**
- Your contrapositive lesson already has animated React visuals (the bulb stage, the flip demo, the rule drill). HyperFrames is HTML plus animation, so those visuals port over with little rework.
- A lecture is narrated, and HyperFrames has a voiceover step. Chase's recipe leaves narration to ElevenLabs, and you have no key.
- It is a maintained toolchain with a checking loop. Chase's is a method you rebuild from his description each time.

**What I haven't verified**
- Nate's HyperFrames segment is only about one minute of a ten-repo roundup. His claims are unverified, and the video itself isn't sponsored.
- The HyperFrames skills are already in this repo (`.claude/skills/hyperframes*`). I don't know yet whether the CLI runs on this machine. I haven't run it.
- For the AI voice, the docs describe a free HeyGen sign-in allowance and an optional local Kokoro voice. I haven't tested either, and I don't know how it sounds. HeyGen sign-in is a login step for you, so I'd hold that until you say so.
- Your answers are saved in the brainstorm file.

Two questions are still open:

[external_agent_tool_result]
1	# Setup and providers — install, auth, RAM ladders, forcing a provider
2	
3	## Setup — install heygen first (free-usage path)
4	
5	Install the HeyGen CLI through its [verified release instructions](https://developers.heygen.com/cli), then run:
6	
7	```bash
8	heygen update             # free usage needs the OAuth-capable CLI (v0.3.0+)
9	heygen auth login --oauth # OAuth = free subscription credits; --api-key bills API credits
10	```
11	
12	This unlocks the FREE path for bgm/sfx/image/icon catalog search, TTS (voice), and avatar videos. Sign in with `--oauth` — the free allowance rides on the OAuth session (an API key bills API credits instead). **media-use requires heygen >= v0.3.0 uniformly** (the OAuth free-usage path needs it), so `--doctor` nudges older CLIs to update even for API-key-only use. Before resolving anything, verify setup with:
13	
14	```bash
15	npx hyperframes media-use resolve --doctor
16	```
17	
18	## Host HeyGen access (no CLI sign-in)
19	
20	A host app can give media-use HeyGen access of its own by setting
21	`HEYGEN_API_BASE` (its gateway), `HEYGEN_API_KEY` (the token that gateway
22	accepts) and, for a loopback gateway, `HEYGEN_ALLOW_HTTP=1`. The gateway adds
23	the host's key, so it never enters the agent's environment, and every
24	call is charged to that key's API credits. The `heygen` CLI honours the same
25	variables, so `resolve` (bgm/sfx/image/icon/voice/avatar-video) and the audio
26	engine's TTS all go through the host. With host access, skip CLI install and
27	sign-in, prefer the host's own HeyGen tools where it has them, never fall back
28	to a local or third-party generator on your own, and relay a refused call's
29	message (for example "add a key in Settings > Account") as written.
30	
31	## Providers
32	
33	media-use holds no keys; every external tool owns its auth. Generation is
34	centered on the HeyGen CLI free-usage path. Install and authenticate `heygen`
35	before resolving bgm/sfx/image/icon/voice/avatar-video; music and sound effects from
36	a host app's own tools need none of it. Local tools are opt-in
37	alternatives where they exist: mflux for image, Kokoro for voice, Parakeet for
38	transcription, and LTX for local video generation. `resolve` spec-checks
39	AVAILABLE RAM for those local ladders (`describeModelLadder`); the agent can
40	see the ladder and override.
41	
42	| Type      | Provider / path                                                                                                                                                               |
43	| --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
44	| bgm/sfx   | heygen catalog free-usage path; a host app's own music and sound tools come first                                                                                             |
45	| image     | heygen search free-usage path; optional local mflux; codex `image_gen` upsell                                                                                                 |
46	| voice     | heygen tts free-usage path; optional local **Kokoro** (free, on-device)                                                                                                       |
47	| icon      | heygen asset search free-usage path                                                                                                                                           |
48	| logo      | theSVG (thesvg.org), then GitHub org avatar, then domain favicon (all free)                                                                                                   |
49	| grade/lut | local core-preset map, params/CDN look index, deterministic `buildCube` fallback                                                                                              |
50	| video     | heygen avatar video free-usage path (sign-in nudge on auth failure); optional ...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

Title: Overview - HeyGen Documentation
URL: https://developers.heygen.com/cli
Source element: <main>
---
Overview
Copy page

Generate AI avatar videos from your terminal with the HeyGen CLI. Authenticate once, then script video creation, translation, and lipsync into any local.

The HeyGen CLI gives developers and AI agents command-line access to HeyGen’s video platform. It wraps the v3 API, outputs structured JSON by default, and works out of the box in scripts, CI pipelines, and agent workflows.
​
1. Install the CLI
curl -fsSL https://static.heygen.ai/cli/install.sh | bash

This installs the latest stable release into ~/.local/bin.
Verify the installation:
heygen --version

The CLI ships as a single binary with no runtime prerequisites. macOS (Apple Silicon and Intel) and Linux (x64 and arm64) are supported. Windows support is coming soon — WSL is recommended in the meantime.
​
2. Authenticate
Log in with your API key from API dashboard:
heygen auth login

Paste your API key when prompted. The key is stored locally at ~/.heygen/credentials.
For CI/Docker/agent environments, set the environment variable instead — it takes precedence over stored credentials:
export HEYGEN_API_KEY=your-api-key

Verify your credentials:
heygen auth status

​
Log in with OAuth
As an alternative to an API key, log in with your HeyGen account via OAuth:
heygen auth login --oauth

This opens your browser to sign in at HeyGen.com.
Logging in one way replaces the other stored credential. Non-interactive shells (piped input, CI=true, or HEYGEN_NONINTERACTIVE=1) default to the API-key flow — pass --oauth explicitly in automation. Set BROWSER=none or HEYGEN_NO_BROWSER=1 to print the sign-in URL instead of opening a browser.
​
3. Create a Video
Send a prompt to the Video Agent and let it handle avatar, voice, and layout:
heygen video-agent create --prompt "A presenter explaining our product launch in 30 seconds"

Output
{
  "data": {
    "session_id": "sess_abc123",
    "status": "generating",
    "video_id": "vid_xyz789",
    "created_at": 1711288320
  }
}

The CLI returns immediately with structured JSON. Your video is generating in the background.
For full control over every parameter, use video create with a JSON body:
heygen video create -d '{
  "type": "avatar",
  "avatar_id": "avt_angela_01",
  "script": "Welcome to our Q4 earnings call.",
  "voice_id": "1bd001e7e50f421d891986aad5e3e5d2"
}'

Use --request-schema on any command to discover the expected JSON fields — no auth required:
heygen video create --request-schema
heygen video-agent create --request-schema

​
4. Check Status
Poll for the result using the video_id returned from step 3:
heygen video get vid_xyz789

Output
{
  "data": {
    "id": "vid_xyz789",
    "title": "Product launch explainer",
    "status": "completed",
    "video_url": "https://files.heygen.com/video/vid_xyz789.mp4",
    "thumbnail_url": "https://files.heygen.com/thumb/vid_xyz789.jpg",
    "duration": 32.5,
    "created_at": 1711288320,
    "completed_at": 1711288452
  }
}

Status moves through pending → processing → completed or failed. If the video fails, the response includes failure_code and failure_message fields.
Tip: Add --wait to the create command to block until the video is ready instead of polling manually. The default timeout is 20 minutes — override with --timeout 30m. On timeout, the CLI exits with code 4 and prints the last known resource state along with a hint to resume polling manually.
​
5. Download the Video
Once complete, download to a local file:
heygen video download vid_xyz789 --output-path ./launch-video.mp4

Output
{
  "asset": "video",
  "message": "Downloaded video to ./launch-video.mp4",
  "path": "./launch-video.mp4"
}

If the video was created with captions enabled, you can download the captioned version:
heygen video download vid_xyz789 --asset captioned --output-path ./launch-captioned.mp4

Output Modes
Next
Ctrl+I
Powered by
This documentation is built and hosted on Mintlify, a develop...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

2	
3	Every student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before
4	writing or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.

12	## The rules these corrections came from
13

15	enforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface

17

20	  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.

27	## Corrections
28	
29	### Contrapositive and arrow lessons: cut the sophistry (2026-10-11)
30	
31	Joshua rejected the first contrapositive lesson as 'terribly done' and then rejected the rewrite for 'a lot of sophistry'. Rules from the two rounds:
32	
33	- **Say what it is first, in plain words, before any example.** Name the idea, say what it states, then show it. The contrapositive is the **same rule said a different way**. The two are **logically equivalent**: if one is true, the other is true, and you can use either. Lead with that. Source: his own teaching, [[contrapositive-teaching]].
34	- **No metaphor-of-obligation language.** Never write that a rule 'makes a promise', that the left side 'depends on' the right, or that it has 'nothing to stand on'. Say what is true and why, in the plainest reasoning: if the left side had happened, the right side would have come with it.
35	- **Keep it natural, intuitive and personable.** Contractions, short sentences, a teacher thinking next to the student. No lecture-hall phrasing. If a sentence sounds like it is trying to sound wise, cut it.
36	- **Name the lesson by its topic.** 'Contrapositive', not 'Write and read the contrapositive'.
37	- **A lesson explains before it drills.** Order for a concept lesson: what it is, see it work, how to do it, what it is not, the steps in short, why it matters. Do not open with an example or a question.
38	- **Must arrow note must say:** what the arrow says, where to start reading (the left), and what the arrow is for. Not just which side is sufficient.
39	- **A card front asks the question, the back gives the answer first.** The answer is the large line. Be specific: 'Can you read a rule from right to left?', not 'Can you reverse the arrow?'. Never 'the other one'.
40	- **Fix the vague word, not the sentence.** 'Reverse' and 'the other one' were both rejected as not specific. Say what the student actually does or what the condition actually is.
41	- **Never call the contrapositive 'the one other thing' a rule tells you.** Joshua (2026-10-11): it is not the only other thing you know. Say the two are equivalent and interchangeable: know one, you know the other. Do not claim a closed list of what else is known.
42	- **Do not use the raffle (win the raffle, get a bicycle) as the example in new contrapositive or arrow material.** Pick a fresh everyday example for each lesson. The contrapositive note now uses an elevator: if it is moving, its doors are closed. The older course text still uses the raffle; replace it as those lessons are revisited.
43	- **Contrapositive lesson (Joshua, 2026-10-11, final): no thought experiment, no guess box, no flip card.** An earlier coat-and-guess card confused him ('I don't know what you did about that coat example'). F...

101	      <blockquote>If you are enrolled in law school, then you are a student.</blockquote>

104	      {attempted && <div className="cr-discovery-reasoning">
105	        <h3>The missing requirement rules out enrollment</h3>
106	        <p>That is the <strong>contrapositive</strong>: <strong>if the necessary condition is false, the sufficient condition must be false too.</strong></p>
107	        <p>Being a student is required for enrollment. When that requirement is missing, enrollment is ruled out. We have not added a new rule; this follows from the rule we already had.</p>
108	        <figure className="cr-contrapositive-pair">
109	          <figcaption>Two ways to read the same rule</figcaption>
110	          <p><strong>Original</strong></p><RuleDiagram left="Enrolled in law school" right="Is a student"/>

112	        </figure>
113	        <p>This is different from simply reversing the arrow. <strong>Being a student does not prove enrollment.</strong> A student could be studying history instead. The contrapositive starts with <strong>not being a student</strong>.</p>

120	function FourRules() {
121	  return <NoteSection title="Try it on four rules">
122	    <p>A rule can have a “not” nowhere, on the right, on the left or on both sides. Every time: flip the sides, put “not” on both ideas, and take off any “not” that was already there. Try one of each.</p>

126	
127	function ContrapositiveNote() {
128	  return <>
129	    <NoteSection title="What a contrapositive is">
130	      <p>You already know the core of every conditional. If the sufficient condition happens, the necessary condition <strong>must</strong> happen. The left side of the arrow guarantees the right side. Every time.</p>
131	      <p>The contrapositive is what you know when the right side of the arrow goes missing:</p>
132	      <p className="ct-definition"><strong>If the <span className="ct-hl">necessary condition</span> is absent, the <span className="ct-hl" data-tone="amber">sufficient condition</span> is absent too.</strong></p>
133	      <p>Take this rule: <strong>If the ceiling light is on, then the power must be on.</strong></p>
134	      <p>So if the light is on, it needs power. It has to have power to come on. The power is completely necessary. Now, what if there's no power? Then the light doesn't have what it needs. 6	**Plugin installs:** Before setup or freshness commands, follow [plugin execution rules](../hyperframes/references/plugin-installation.md) when this skill is inside a HyperFrames plugin. Standalone installs keep the update instructions below.
7	
8	# HyperFrames Core
9	
10	**Agent pitfalls (read first):**
11	
12	- Center with flex/`inset`, not CSS `transform: translate(-50%,-50%)` on a node you then GSAP `x`/`y`. Lint: `gsap_css_transform_conflict`. Use `fromTo` or `xPercent`/`yPercent`.
13	- Do not add a scene-exit `tl.set(..., {visibility:"hidden"})`. The runtime already hides timed clips. Opacity fades on inner nodes (or `opacity` on `.clip`) are enough. Caption hard-kills are a different rule.
14	- `window.__timelines["id"]` must match the root `data-composition-id`.
15	- After `render`, read the summary's second line: `beginframe` vs `screenshot`, GPU mode, stage timings. `screenshot` + `software gpu` on Linux is the slow path.
16	
17	HyperFrames renders video from HTML. A composition is an HTML file whose DOM declares timing with `data-*` attributes, whose animation runtime is seekable, and whose media playback is owned by the framework.
18	
19	This skill is the **technical contract** — how to build one hyperframes project. The body below is the build guide; per-topic detail lives in `references/` (index next), read on demand. Process docs (brief, storyboard, review, production, dispatch, frame-worker) live in `/hyperframes` → `references/`. Other concerns live in the sibling domain skills — `hyperframes-animation`, `hyperframes-creative`, `media-use`, `hyperframes-cli`, `hyperframes-registry`. 1	# Variables and Media
2	
3	Two separate concerns, grouped because both control "what flows in from outside the HTML": runtime parameters (variables) and external media files (video/audio).
4	
5	## Variables
6	
7	Declare variables on the `<html>` element with `data-composition-variables`. Each declaration needs `id`, `type`, `label`, and `default`:
8	
9	```html
10	<html
11	  data-composition-variables='[
12	    {"id":"title","type":"string","label":"Title","default":"Hello"},
13	    {"id":"accent","type":"color","label":"Accent","default":"#66d9ef"}
14	  ]'
15	></html>
16	```
17	
18	**Prefer declarative bindings — no script needed** for direct substitution:
19	
20	```html
21	<img class="clip" data-start="0" data-duration="5" data-var-src="heroImage" src="fallback.jpg" />
22	<h1 class="clip" data-start="0" data-duration="5" data-var-text="title">Fallback</h1>
23	<style>
24	  .card {
25	    color: var(--accent);
26	  }
27	</style>
28	```
29	
30	- `data-var-src="id"` substitutes the element's `src` (URL string or image `{url}`); the authored `src` is the fallback.
31	- `data-var-text="id"` substitutes the element's own text; element children (nested clips, animated spans) are preserved.
32	- Every scalar variable is applied automatically as a `--{id}` CSS custom property on the composition root, so `var(--id)` CSS responds to overrides — no `setProperty` boilerplate.
33	- Bindings resolve identically in preview and render, and per-instance for sub-compositions.
34	- Caveat: media with audio should keep a real fallback `src` — render audio extraction reads the authored attribute (lint: `media_variable_src_no_fallback`).
35	
36	For logic beyond direct substitution (loops, conditionals, derived values), read values once during initialization:
37	
38	```js
39	const { title, accent } = window.__hyperframes.getVariables();
40	document.getElementById("title").textContent = title;
41	```
42	
43	### Variable Rules
44	
45	- Supported types and their extra options (consumed by Studio's editing UI):
46	  - `string` — optional `placeholder`, `maxLength`
47	  - `number` — optional `min`, `max`, `step`, `unit`
48	  - `color` — none
49	  - `boolean` — none
50	  - `enum` — **required** `options: [{ "value": "...", "label": "..." }, ...]`
51	- Always provide useful `default` values so preview works without CLI overrides.
52	- Use `data-variable-values='{"title":"Pro"}'` on sub-composition hosts for per-instance overrides.
53	- Use `npx hyperframes render --variables '{"title":"Q4 Report"}'` or `--variables-file` for render-time overrides.
54	- Add `--strict-variables` in CI: turns undeclared keys, type mismatches, and enum values not in `options` into errors instead of warnings.
55	- Read values once during init, not on every animation tick — variables don't change mid-render.
56	- Media color grading can use exact variable references inside `data-color-grading` JSON. Use `$gradingPreset` or `${gradingIntensity}` as the whole field value; the runtime resolves it from the current composition's variables before applying shader adjustments, finishing details, blur/pixelate effects, and custom LUTs.
57	
58	### Two JSON Shapes (Easy to Confuse)
59	
60	- `data-composition-variables` is an **array of declarations** (the schema): `[{id, type, label, default}, ...]`
61	- `--variables` and `data-variable-values` are **objects keyed by id** (the values): `{"title":"Q4","accent":"#fff"}`
62	
63	## Media
64	
65	**`<video>`/`<audio>` work at any nesting depth, including inside a sub-composition `<template>` or a wrapper `<div>`.** The runtime discovers media with a flat `document.querySelectorAll("video, audio")`, resolves each element's host composition via `element.closest("[data-composition-id]")`, and rebases its local `data-start` by the accumulated absolute start of every ancestor composition. A host at `2` with child media at `2` therefore starts that media at root time `4`, consistently in preview, snapshot, extraction, and render. Legacy projects t...

1	# Determinism, Animation Runtime, and Layout
2	
3	HyperFrames seeks compositions frame-by-frame. Every frame must be reproducible from its time value alone — same input time → same pixels. Three contracts enforce this: the **animation runtime contract**, the **determinism rules**, and the **layout contract**.
4	
5	## Animation Runtime Contract
6	
7	GSAP is the primary runtime. The core requirement is generic: animation state must be seekable from HyperFrames time.
8	
9	For GSAP:
10	
11	- Use `gsap.timeline({ paused: true })`.
12	- Register it on `window.__timelines["<composition-id>"]`, keyed by the composition root's `data-composition-id`. You do **not** need to write `window.__timelines = window.__timelines || {}` first: the runtime creates the registry before your inline scripts evaluate.
13	- **Building inside an async callback is supported.** `document.fonts.ready(...)` and friends are the documented setup path. What you must not do is **register the key before the build finishes**. An empty timeline registered early is treated as ready and nested empty, so the animation renders blank (`lint`: `gsap_timeline_registered_before_async_build`, error). Assign `window.__timelines[id] = tl` at the **end** of the callback, after the tweens are added, and optionally call `window.__hfForceTimelineRebind()` right after.
14	- If the key does not match the root's `data-composition-id`, the runtime still binds it **when it is the only registered timeline**. With two or more registered, a mismatched key leaves the render frozen at t=0.
15	- **Do not** call `tl.play()` for render-critical motion.
16	- **Do not** create empty tweens only to set duration; use `data-duration` on the clip instead.
17	
18	Use the `hyperframes-animation` skill for tween syntax, position parameters, eases, and performance rules. Non-GSAP duration inference lives in `hyperframes-animation/adapters/`.
19	
20	## Determinism Rules
21	
22	Rendered frames must be reproducible from the requested time. Do **not** use any of the following for visual state:
23	
24	- `Date.now()`, `performance.now()`, or any render-time clock.
25	- Unseeded `Math.random()`. Use a seeded PRNG if random-looking placement is needed.
26	- Render-time network fetches for required assets. Inline or pre-bundle them.
27	- Hover, scroll, pointer, or focus state. The renderer has no input events.
28	- Unbounded infinite loops. `repeat: -1` is allowed only when the root declares a finite `data-duration` — deterministic seeking and export clip to that explicit window (`gsap_infinite_repeat` demotes to a warning). Without a finite composition duration it stays a hard error: the timeline can report an unbounded length and render planning fails. When the loop itself must end **before** the composition does, compute a finite count: `repeat: Math.max(0, Math.floor(duration / cycleDuration) - 1)` — **`floor`, not `ceil`** (`ceil` overshoots `data-duration` and trips the `gsap_repeat_ceil_overshoot` lint; `max(0, …)` avoids a negative repeat = infinite).
29	
30	Also avoid:
31	
32	- Tweening `display`, raw `visibility`, or `autoAlpha` **on a clip element**: HyperFrames timing owns a clip's visibility, and `lint` rejects it (`gsap_animates_clip_element`). Fade with `opacity`, or tween a child wrapper. Do not tween `class="clip"`.
33	- There is no fixed allowlist of animatable properties. `lint` enforces a **denylist**, so `filter`, `clipPath`, `strokeDashoffset`, `width`, `height` and similar are all legitimate targets. Prefer transforms and opacity where you have the choice, for performance rather than correctness. The per-runtime detail lives in `hyperframes-animation/adapters/`.
34	- Animating the same property on the same element from multiple timelines at the same time — GSAP's overwrite behavior is order-dependent and can flip between renders.
35	
36	## Layout Contract
37	
38	Build the visible end-state in static HTML and CSS first, then animate from/to that state.
39	
40	- The composition root has...

Author display at **weight 400** (700 reads as a heavy bold, off-register), and treat italic as the browser-synthesized slant (acceptable for the pull-quote register; add a real italic face only if a project leans hard on it). ## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Generate WebVTT captions from cue times
command: cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/contrapositive-video/lecture && mkdir -p renders && python - <<'EOF'
import json, re
cues = json.load(open('cues.json', encoding='utf-8'))['cues']
def ts(t):
    h=int(t//3600); m=int(t%3600//60); s=t%60
    return f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}:{s:06.3f}"
out=["WEBVTT",""]
n=0
for c in cues:
    # split a sentence group into phrases at sentence ends and colons/commas when long
    parts=re.findall(r'[^.!?]+[.!?]+|[^.!?]+$', c['text'])
    parts=[p.strip() for p in parts if p.strip()]
    # speech inside a cue is continuous; allocate time by character count
    total=sum(len(p) for p in parts)
    dur=c['end']-c['start']; t=c['start']
    for p in parts:
        d=dur*len(p)/total
        n+=1
        out += [str(n), f"{ts(t)} --> {ts(t+d)}", p, ""]
        t+=d
open('renders/lecture.en.vtt','w',encoding='utf-8').write("\n".join(out))
print(n,'cues')
EOF
head -12 renders/lecture.en.vtt

**What it shows, in order**
1. The rule: light on, so power must be on.
2. The bulb story: the power goes out, the light dies, "No power, no light." That is the contrapositive hiding inside the rule.
3. The definition, with "necessary" in teal and "sufficient" in amber, matching the lesson page.
4. Flip the sides, then put a not on both, drawn as a red diagonal line through each idea.
5. "Logically equivalent" and "Interchangeable!"
6. The wrong versions (reverse and inverse) as red "Wrong" cards, next to a green "Correct" one.
7. A three-step recap, ending on "Now try it on four rules".

- **Build and audio:** the build check passed with no errors, and the render finished with audio present at normal loudness.

import { useId, useState } from 'react';
import { sufficientConditionFilms } from '../content/sufficientConditionFilms';

export type SufficientConditionFilmAsset = { id: string; title: string; src: string; poster?: string; captions: string; caption: string; transcript: string };

/** Render only an actual generated asset; the written lesson remains available without video. */
export function SufficientConditionFilm({ assets = sufficientConditionFilms, title = 'What the rule gives us', ariaLabel = 'Short explanations of sufficiency' }: { assets?: SufficientConditionFilmAsset[]; title?: string; ariaLabel?: string }) {
  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);
  const [failedId, setFailedId] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0);
  const videoId = useId();
  const asset = assets[selected];
  if (!asset) return null;
  const failed = failedId === asset.id;
  return <section className="sc-film" aria-label={ariaLabel}>
    <header><p className="sc-eyebrow">Watch the explanation</p><h3>{title}</h3></header>
    {assets.length > 1 && <div className="sc-film-selector" role="group" aria-label="Choose an explanation">
      {assets.map((clip, index) => <button type="button" key={clip.id}
        aria-pressed={index === selected} aria-controls={videoId}
        onClick={() => { setSelected(index); setFailedId(null); }}>{clip.title}</button>)}
    </div>}
    <figure>
      <video id={videoId} key={`${asset.id}-${attempt}`} controls preload="metadata" playsInline
        poster={asset.poster} aria-label={asset.caption} onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}>
        <source src={asset.src} type="video/mp4" onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}/>
        <track kind="captions" src={asset.captions} srcLang="en" label="English" default/>
        Your browser cannot play this clip. The explanation is available below.
      </video>
      <figcaption>{asset.caption}</figcaption>
    </figure>
    {failed && <div className="sc-film-error">
      <p role="alert">The video couldn't load. You can read the explanation below or try again.</p>
      <button type="button" onClick={() => { setFailedId(null); setAttempt(value => value + 1); }}>Try loading the video again</button>
    </div>}
    <details key={`transcript-${asset.id}-${failed}`} open={failed || undefined}>
      <summary>Read the video explanation</summary><p>{asset.transcript}</p>
    </details>
  </section>;
}

command: cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components && cat >> ContrapositiveVisuals.tsx <<'EOF'

const LECTURE_TRANSCRIPT = "Here's a rule: if the ceiling light is on, then the power must be on. Now flip the question. What if the power goes out? Could the light still be on? Nope. No power, no light. That's a second rule, and it was hiding inside the first one. If the power is off, then the light must be off. That's the contrapositive. If the necessary condition is absent, the sufficient condition is absent too. Here's how you write it. Start with the rule: Light, arrow, Power. Step one: flip the sides. Power goes on the left, and Light goes on the right. Step two: put a not on both. Not power, arrow, not light. Read it back: if there's no power, the light is not on. The original and the contrapositive are logically equivalent. If one is true, the other is true, so you can use either one. They're interchangeable! Be careful, though. Only flipping the sides is not the contrapositive, and neither is only adding a not. Each one is a different rule, and the original doesn't give it to you. You need both changes together. So when you see a rule, flip it, put a not on both sides, and you've got the contrapositive. Now try it on the next few rules yourself.";

/** Short narrated lecture. The written lesson below stands on its own if the video cannot load. */
export function ContrapositiveLecture() {
  const [failed, setFailed] = useState(false);
  return <figure className="ct-lecture">
    <video controls preload="metadata" playsInline poster="/media/conditionals/contrapositive/lecture.jpg" aria-label="A short lecture on the contrapositive" onError={() => setFailed(true)}>
      <source src="/media/conditionals/contrapositive/lecture.mp4" type="video/mp4" onError={() => setFailed(true)}/>
      <track kind="captions" src="/media/conditionals/contrapositive/lecture.vtt" srcLang="en" label="English"/>
      Your browser cannot play this video. The lesson is written out below.
    </video>
    {failed && <p role="alert" className="ct-lecture-error">The video couldn't load. The lesson is written out below.</p>}
    <details open={failed || undefined}><summary>Read the video</summary><p>{LECTURE_TRANSCRIPT}</p></details>
  </figure>;
}
EOF

.ct-lecture{margin:1.2rem 0 1.6rem;padding:0}
.ct-lecture video{display:block;width:100%;aspect-ratio:16/9;border-radius:20px;background:#0f2a38;box-shadow:0 20px 40px -24px #0f2a38}
.ct-lecture details{margin-top:.8rem;font-size:.95rem}
.ct-lecture summary{cursor:pointer;font-weight:700}
.ct-lecture-error{margin-top:.8rem}
EOF

it('defines the contrapositive first, then says it was already there, plays the four worlds, flips and negates, and sets it against the reverse and inverse', () => {
  render(<OpeningCourseContent id="contrapositive"/>);
  const names = ['What a contrapositive is', 'You already knew this', "Which world can't exist?", 'How to write it', 'What it is not', "Read it, don't always write it", "Check that you've got it"];
  for (const name of names) expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name })).toBeVisible();
  const full = document.body.textContent ?? '';
  const text = full.slice(0, full.indexOf('Try it on four rules'));
  const order = names.map(name => text.indexOf(name));
  expect(order).toEqual([...order].sort((m, n) => m - n));
  expect(text.indexOf('If the necessary condition is absent, the sufficient condition is absent too')).toBeLessThan(text.indexOf('ceiling light'));
  expect(text).toMatch(/then the power must be on/);
  expect(text).toMatch(/left side of the arrow/);
  expect(text).toMatch(/right side of the arrow/);
  expect(text).toMatch(/logically equivalent/);
  expect(text).toMatch(/waiting for you to spot it/);
  expect(text).toMatch(/interchangeable!/);
  expect(text).not.toMatch(/foreign|promise|nothing to stand on|depends on|raffle|elevator|coat|coffee|breathing|Logic Games|the one other thing|ignore the conditional/i);
  expect(text).not.toMatch(/[—–]/);
  expect(text).not.toMatch(/~/);
  expect(text).toMatch(/If the power is off, then the ceiling light must be off/);
  expect(document.querySelectorAll('.ct-neg').length).toBeGreaterThan(8);
});

it('asks one clear question and lets the student find the one world the rule forbids', () => {
  render(<OpeningCourseContent id="contrapositive"/>);
  const picker = document.querySelector('.ct-picker') as HTMLElement;
  expect(within(picker).getByText(/Which ONE of these four worlds can't happen?/)).toBeInTheDocument();
  expect(picker.dataset.found).toBe('false');
  fireEvent.click(within(picker).getByRole('button', { name: 'Light off, power on' }));
  expect(picker.dataset.found).toBe('false');
  expect(within(picker).getByRole('status').textContent).toMatch(/Not that one. That world is allowed/);
  fireEvent.click(within(picker).getByRole('button', { name: 'Light on, power off' }));
  expect(picker.dataset.found).toBe('true');
  expect(within(picker).getByRole('status').textContent).toMatch(/Can't happen!/);
  expect(picker.querySelectorAll('[data-verdict=allowed]')).toHaveLength(3);
  expect(picker.querySelectorAll('[data-verdict=broken]')).toHaveLength(1);
});

it('says the contrapositive in plain English early, pulses the key term, and no longer repeats the raffle recap', () => {

});

new='''function ContrapositiveNote() {
  return <>
    <NoteSection title="What a contrapositive is">
      <p>If the sufficient condition happens, the necessary condition <strong>must</strong> happen. The left side of the arrow guarantees the right side.</p>
      <p>The contrapositive is what you know when the right side of the arrow goes missing:</p>
      <p className="ct-definition"><strong>If the <span className="ct-hl">necessary condition</span> is absent, the <span className="ct-hl" data-tone="amber">sufficient condition</span> is absent too.</strong></p>
      <p>Take this rule: <strong>If the ceiling light is on, then the power must be on.</strong> With no power, the light can't be on. No power, no light. As a rule: <strong>If the power is off, then the ceiling light must be off.</strong></p>
    </NoteSection>
    <NoteSection title="You already knew this">
      <p>The contrapositive was inside the original rule the whole time, waiting for you to spot it. That's why the two are <PulseWord>logically equivalent</PulseWord>: if one is true, the other is true, and you can use either. They're <PunchWord>interchangeable!</PunchWord></p>
    </NoteSection>
    <NoteSection title="Watch the lesson">
      <ContrapositiveLecture/>
    </NoteSection>
    <NoteSection title="How to write it">
      <p>Write the rule with an arrow: <strong>Light → Power</strong>. The sufficient condition goes on the left side of the arrow. The necessary condition goes on the right side of the arrow.</p>
      <p>The contrapositive is <strong><Neg>Power</Neg> → <Neg>Light</Neg></strong>. A line through an idea means “not.” Flip the sides, put “not” on both, and take off any “not” that was already there:</p>
      <FlipAndNegateDemo/>
    </NoteSection>
    <NoteSection title="What it is not">
      <p>Two other changes look like the contrapositive. Each one gives you a different rule that the original does not guarantee. Wrong answer choices use them on purpose.</p>
      <ThreeMoves/>
    </NoteSection>
    <NoteSection title="Read it, don't always write it">
      <p>You can read a rule backward without writing anything: go from right to left and put a “not” on everything. Write the contrapositive when it helps you see the rule or connect it to another rule.</p>
    </NoteSection>
  </>;
}

'''

import { useEffect, useRef, useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';
import { Check, RotateCcw, X, Zap, ZapOff } from 'lucide-react';
import './contrapositive-visuals.css';

/** True once the element has scrolled into view. Without IntersectionObserver (old browsers, tests) it is true straight away. */
function useInView<T extends Element>(): [React.RefObject<T>, boolean] {
  const ref = useRef<T>(null);
  const [inView, setInView] = useState(typeof IntersectionObserver === 'undefined');
  useEffect(() => {
    const node = ref.current;
    if (!node || typeof IntersectionObserver === 'undefined') return;
    const observer = new IntersectionObserver(entries => { if (entries.some(entry => entry.isIntersecting)) { setInView(true); observer.disconnect(); } }, { threshold: 0.6 });
    observer.observe(node);
    return () => observer.disconnect();
  }, []);
  return [ref, inView];
}


/** An idea shown as false: struck through from its top left corner to its bottom right, with a hidden "not" for screen readers. */
export function Neg({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  return <span className="ct-neg"><span className="ct-sr">not </span>{children}</span>;
}

/** Plain text where a leading ~ on a letter code (like ~W or ~B) is drawn as the struck idea instead of a squiggle. */
export function Notation({ text }: { text: string }) {
  return <>{text.split(/(~[A-Za-z]+)/g).map((part, index) => part.startsWith('~') ? <Neg key={index}>{part.slice(1)}</Neg> : part)}</>;
}

/** A word that punches the screen once, when it scrolls into view, then keeps a steady glow. */
export function PunchWord({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  const [ref, inView] = useInView<HTMLSpanElement>();
  return <span ref={ref} className="ct-punch" data-in-view={inView}>{children}</span>;
}

/** A key term that keeps breathing: a glowing highlight that swells and settles for as long as the page is open. */
export function PulseWord({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  return <span className="ct-pulse-word">{children}</span>;
}

export function RuleDrill() {
  const [step, setStep] = useState(0);
  const [wrong, setWrong] = useState<number[]>([]);
  const [solved, setSolved] = useState<number | null>(null);
  const rule = ruleCases[step];
  const choices = choicesFor(rule);
  const last = step === ruleCases.length - 1;
  const lastTried = wrong.length ? choices[wrong[wrong.length - 1]] : null;
  const message = solved !== null
    ? rule.why.contrapositive
    : lastTried ? `Not that one. ${rule.why[lastTried.kind as 'reverse' | 'inverse']} Try another.` : 'Pick the diagram that says the same thing as the rule.';
  const next = () => { setStep(value => value + 1); setWrong([]); setSolved(null); };
  const restart = () => { setStep(0); setWrong([]); setSolved(null); };
  return <div className="ct-drill" data-step={step}>
    <div className="ct-drill-top">
      <p className="ct-picker-kicker">Your turn</p>
      <p className="ct-drill-count">Rule {step + 1} of {ruleCases.length}<span className="ct-drill-dots" aria-hidden="true">{ruleCases.map((item, index) => <i key={item.tag} data-state={index < step ? 'done' : index === step ? 'now' : 'todo'}/>)}</span></p>
    </div>
    <p className="ct-drill-tag">{rule.tag}</p>
    <p className="ct-drill-sentence">{rule.sentence}</p>
    <div className="ct-drill-original" role="img" aria-label={`The rule: ${spoken(rule.left)} implies ${spoken(rule.right)}`}>
      <Pill code={rule.left.code} tone="amber" negated={rule.left.negated}/><span className="ct-drill-arrow" aria-hidden="true">→</span><Pill code={rule.right.code} tone="teal" negated={rule.right.negated}/>
    </div>
    <ul className="ct-drill-legend">
      <li><Pill code={rule.left.code} tone="amber" negated={false}/> {rule.left.meaning}</li>
      <li><Pill code={rule.right.code} tone="teal" negated={false}/> {rule.right.meaning}</li>
    </ul>
    <p className="ct-picker-question" id={`ct-drill-q-${step}`}>Which one is the contrapositive?</p>
    <div className="ct-drill-options" role="group" aria-labelledby={`ct-drill-q-${step}`}>
      {choices.map((choice, index) => {
        const state = solved === index ? 'right' : wrong.includes(index) ? 'wrong' : 'open';
        return <button key={choice.kind} type="button" className="ct-drill-option" data-state={state} disabled={solved !== null && solved !== index} aria-label={`${spoken(choice.left)} → ${spoken(choice.right)}`} onClick={() => { if (solved !== null) return; if (choice.kind === 'contrapositive') setSolved(index); else setWrong
          <span aria-hidden="true" className="ct-drill-diagram"><Pill code={choice.left.code} tone={choice.leftTone} negated={choice.left.negated}/><span className="ct-drill-arrow">→</span><Pill code={choice.right.code} tone={choice.rightTone} negated={choice.right.negated}/></span>
          {state === 'right' && <span className="ct-drill-mark" data-kind="right"><Check size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden="true"/>Yes</span>}
          {state === 'wrong' && <span className="ct-drill-mark" data-kind="wrong"><X size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden="true"/>No</span>}
        </button>;
      })}
    </div>
    <p className="ct-picker-message" role="status" data-solved={solved !== null}>{message}</p>
    {solved !== null && (last
      ? <div className="ct-drill-end"><strong>That is all four. Same two steps every time.</strong><button type="button" className="ct-drill-next" onClick={restart}><RotateCcw size={15} aria-hidden="true"/>Try them again</button></div>
      : <button type="button" className="ct-drill-next" onClick={next}>Next rule</button>)}
  </div>;
}

it('works through four fresh rules one at a time, one for each place a not can sit', () => {
  render(<OpeningCourseContent id="contrapositive"/>);
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Try it on four rules' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(document.body.textContent).not.toMatch(/patterns?/i);
  const drill = document.querySelector('.ct-drill') as HTMLElement;
  const rules = [
    ['If a guest is on the list, the guest gets a wristband.', 'not W → not L'],
    ['If the shop is open, the shutters are not down.', 'D → not O'],
    ['If the gate is not locked, the dog can get out.', 'not G → L'],
    ['If a ticket is not stamped, it is not valid.', 'V → S'],
  ];
  rules.forEach(([sentence, correct], index) => {
    expect(within(drill).getByText(sentence)).toBeVisible();
    expect(within(drill).getByText('Rule ' + (index + 1) + ' of 4', { exact: false })).toBeVisible();
    const options = [...drill.querySelectorAll<HTMLButtonElement>('.ct-drill-option')];
    expect(options).toHaveLength(3);
    const wrong = options.find(option => option.getAttribute('aria-label') !== correct)!;
    fireEvent.click(wrong);
    expect(wrong.dataset.state).toBe('wrong');
    expect(within(drill).getByRole('status').textContent).toMatch(/Not that one/);
    fireEvent.click(within(drill).getByRole('button', { name: correct }));
    expect(within(drill).getByRole('button', { name: correct }).dataset.state).toBe('right');
    if (index < 3) fireEvent.click(within(drill).getByRole('button', { name: 'Next rule' }));
  });
  expect(within(drill).getByText(/That is all four/)).toBeVisible();
});

it('marks the reverse and the inverse as wrong and the contrapositive as right', () => {
  render(<OpeningCourseContent id="contrapositive"/>);
  const cards = [...document.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('.ct-move')];
  expect(cards.map(card => card.dataset.verdict)).toEqual(['wrong', 'wrong', 'right']);
  expect(cards.map(card => card.querySelector('.ct-move-badge')?.textContent)).toEqual(['Wrong', 'Wrong', 'Right']);
  expect(cards.map(card => card.querySelector('.ct-move-how')?.textContent)).toEqual(['Flip the sides only.', 'Add “not” only.', 'Flip the sides and add “not” to both.']);
});

it('never shows a squiggle anywhere on the contrapositive page, including the practice answer choices', () => {
  render(<OpeningCourseContent id="contrapositive"/>);
  expect(document.body.textContent).not.toMatch(/[~∼¬]/);
  expect(document.querySelectorAll('.ct-drill-option .ct-pill[data-not=true]').length).toBeGreaterThan(3);
});

**What I removed:** the "Each rule has its 'not' in a different place…" paragraph and the "No 'not' in the rule" tag line above each question. The drill starts straight at the rule.

**The new effect:** it's a new one, not used elsewhere on this page.
- **When the title scrolls into view:** each letter springs up one after another, with a little bounce and tilt, instead of the whole word fading in.
- **Afterwards:** a band of amber light keeps sweeping across the dark letters, so it reads as a spotlight passing over.
- **Reduced motion:** with that setting on, both the pop and the sweep switch off and the word is plain dark text.

## Workflow

# Bootcamp Builder

Turn a topic + content into a shipped, repo-native LSAT U bootcamp that matches Intro to LR quality. This is the productized version of the Intro to LR (Main Conclusion) build process. It replaces `lsat-u-topic-builder`.

**The template is the code, not a doc:** resolve the vault root from its `AGENTS.md`, then follow its application routing to `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/src/bootcamps/main-conclusion/` (the Intro to LR build). Read the app's own instructions and the template before scaffolding anything. If inspection n

```
src/bootcamps/{slug}/
  App.tsx, routes.tsx        # nested <BootcampRoutes/> mounted under /bootcamp/{slug}/*
  components/                # workspace-shell (chrome), drills, pickers, coach's note, command palette, trap-master, smart-hints...
  content/*.source.ts        # authored content as typed TS sources + schemas.ts
  data/*.generated.json      # generated from sources via scripts/{slug}/import-content.ts
  modules/                   # lessons, reference, drills, practice-drills, simulator, hard-sentences, diagnostics, journal, settings, ModuleIndex
  persistence/               # Persistence interface, LocalStorage/IndexedDB/SupabaseSynced, slug-prefixed keys
  hooks/ lib/ styles/ types/
  __tests__/impeccable-bans.test.ts
```

command: cat > /tmp/gold.md <<'EOF'


## Gold standard: the Contrapositive lesson page (approved 2026-10-12)

Joshua called the finished page "perfect". Treat it as the template for every other concept lecture: same order, same density, same rules. Do not copy its wording or its examples. Rework the structure for the new topic and pick fresh examples.

**Where it lives:** `ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/` (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx` `ContrapositiveNote` and `FourRules`, `ContrapositiveVisuals.tsx`, `contrapositive-visuals.css`), commit `383747e9` plus the uncommitted follow-ups of 2026-10-12. Video project: `ai-os-projects/contrapositive-video/lecture` (`script.json`, `index.html`, `build_audio.py`). Full correction log: `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md`.

### Page shape (about 600 words of text, seven sections)

1. **What it is.** Plain definition first, in a navy panel, then one short example. No run-up.
2. **Why it matters or what it gives you.** Two or three sentences. The key term pulses, one exclamation at most.
3. **Watch the lesson.** A 60 to 90 second narrated video placed after the idea is introduced. Captions track, poster image, written fallback that only renders when opened or when the video fails.
4. **How to write it.** Two bold steps (Step 1 and Step 2), then an interactive demo with "Step 1. Click here to ..." and "Step 2. Click here to ..." buttons. A second demo uses a fresh, simple, intuitive example where one side already starts negated, so the rule that two nots cancel is shown, not stated. Each demo shows the rule as an if-then sentence above the diagram and the result as an if-then sentence below.
5. **What it is not.** One line, then slim cards: red "Wrong" for each look-alike, green "Right" for the answer. Badge, one-line change, diagram. Nothing else.
6. **A short habit paragraph.** One paragraph, no list.
7. **Pop Quiz.** Centered title with a letter-pop and light-sweep effect. Four items, one at a time. Ideas shown as short phrases, never letters. Right answer explanation is one sentence (the answer as a natural if-then). Wrong answer explanation is "Not that one." plus one specific reason about that choice. No intro paragraph, no category tag lines.

### What Joshua wanted (keep)

- One main example carried through the page, plus one fresh second example. Never the same example in more places than that, and never an example already used on another page.
- Natural if-then sentences for every rule and result. Diagrams support the sentence; they never replace it.
- Bold numbered steps. Controls say what to click. The student drives; nothing autoplays.
- Colour carries meaning and travels with the idea (amber sufficient, teal necessary). Wrong looks wrong (red, X); right looks right (green, check).
- Sleek, high-end visuals. A new effect for each new moment; never reuse the same trick. Every animation has a still equivalent and stops under reduced motion.
- Short. When a page feels like too much, cut sections, not words: remove whole parts that repeat what the video, a demo or the quiz already does.
- Ideas in quiz diagrams are condensed statements ("On the list", "Wristband"), not letter codes.
- Narrated video in his voice standard, AI voice acceptable, built from the same wording as the page.

### What Joshua rejected (never repeat)

- A squiggle (~) for negation anywhere. Use the word "not" or a diagonal strike from the idea's top left to bottom right.
- The raffle, coat, coffee, breathing, elevator, door and any example already used elsewhere. "Door", "patterns" and "move" as student words.
- Sophistry and obligation metaphors ("promise", "depends on", "nothing to stand on"). Stock encouragement and run-ups ("Here's the good news", "Every time:").
- Content repeated on the page, a legend that restates what a pill already says, a paragraph before the quiz explaining the quiz, tag lines that add nothing.
- Complicated interactives: the first light-bulb stage (wires, chips, extra buttons) and the "four worlds" picker were both cut as too busy. One idea, one control, one sentence.
- Long answer explanations. Explanations that talk about the diagram ("both ideas get a not") instead of saying the answer in plain English.
- Letters standing in for ideas.

### Process that produced it (work backwards from this)

1. Write the section list above for the new topic before any copy. Cut anything the video, a demo or the quiz already covers.
2. Draft prose in Joshua's voice, then run the `humanizer` skill over it and the corrections log. No em dashes.
3. Build the visuals one at a time, check each on the live preview (not only in tests), and ask what is new about each effect.
4. Pin the page order and the key phrases in the content test; update the test when a section is deliberately cut.
5. Video: one script file, narration synthesized per sentence, word timings for sync, scenes built in the page's own palette, rendered and checked frame by frame. Voice: Kokoro af_heart (author grade A) when no paid voice key exists.
6. Log every new correction in `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` and here before moving on.

# Session Handoff — Contrapositive lecture video, page rebuild, and saved quality standard

## Where it started
Joshua picked up from the earlier handoff (Contrapositive lesson, video interview open). He chose HyperFrames over Chase AI's method and wanted a code-rendered lecture video with an AI voice, then put it on the Contrapositive page. The session became a long page edit: remove clutter, add flip and negate steps, add a double-negative demo, rebuild the quiz. He then asked that this finished page become the standard for all lectures.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **Video:** 77 s, 1080p, narrated by Kokoro `af_heart`, chosen from the model author's grades (A) and not by ear. It is on the page at `public/media/conditionals/contrapositive/lecture.mp4` with `.vtt` captions and `.jpg` poster. It is built in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os-projects\contrapositive-video\lecture\` (`script.json`, `index.html`, `build_audio.py`, `align_words.py`). HyperFrames 0.8.149 and Kokoro are installed; Kokoro lives in `...\contrapositive-video\.venv` and needs `HYPERFRAMES_PYTHON` set. The HeyGen CLI has no Windows build and was not used.
- **Page order now:** What a contrapositive is, You already knew this, Watch the lesson, How to write it (bold Step 1 flip, Step 2 negate, double negatives cancel, two click demos), What it is not (slim cards), Read it, don't always write it, Pop Quiz. Removed from the page (code kept or deleted as noted): four-worlds picker, bulb stage, pairs table, check questions, negate-the-whole-condition, practical questions, two-rooms lab. `WholeConditionNegation` was deleted.
- **Demos:** the first uses Light/Power. The second is Snowing → Hot with one side starting negated. Both show the rule and the result as if-then sentences.
- **Pop Quiz:** centered title with a letter-pop and light-sweep effect, ideas as short phrases not letters, and one-sentence feedback.
- **Standard saved** in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\.claude\skills\bootcamp-builder\references\teaching-preferences.md` (also `...\skills\claude\bootcamp-builder\references\...`), `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\wiki\COPY-CORRECTIONS.md`, and memory `C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\projects\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\memory\contrapositive-page-is-the-lesson-gold-standard.md` (indexed in `MEMORY.md`).
- **Pushed:** commits `383747e9` and `1f976f3e` on `claude/conditional-bootcamp-drills-2026-10-11`. Only my files were included.
